# EXPANSION CW125-06 — The Cold Took Them

## A prose-first memorial expansion about the remembered claim, the people who inherit it, and the blanks that remain human.

### Prose Wave 125: What a Warning Cannot Finish

## Batch brief

**Content type:** original game-content proposal with full scene, attributable writing, conversation, and conditional-return drafts.
**Content bank:** 48 beat pairings; four alternative authored forms for each pairing; 192 candidates, of which a verified consumer should select only what it can support.
**Current local anchor:** memorial_power_failure/cold_took_them — Power Failure Victims.
**Tone:** A memorial that joins a failed generator, an attempted repair, and a tragic outcome without establishing the technical cause or turning grief into an engineering lesson.
**Scope:** narrative prose only; no production code, authoritative JSON edits, mechanics, route, quest, flag, simulation, or save change.

## 1. Expansion thesis

This plan explores A memorial that joins a failed generator, an attempted repair, and a tragic outcome without establishing the technical cause or turning grief into an engineering lesson. It uses the source text “The generator failed, and the cold took them. We tried to repair it, but it was too late. Their deaths remind us: never neglect the machines that keep us alive.” as a narrow canon anchor, not as an excuse to manufacture witnesses or explain every silence. Six movements and forty-eight beat pairings explore how different present readers may respond to the wording. The proposed passages remain optional editorial drafts until a current owner and route are verified.

## 2. Story question

How can readers feel the danger and regret in this account while refusing to invent machine details, blame, repair procedure, or a lesson that simplifies the dead?

## 5. Human center

The memorial’s movement from machine failure to cold, then its attempt to make the deaths a warning to the living.
 The deceased or collective subject must never become a device whose only purpose is to teach a clean lesson to the living. Keep the person or people larger than the inscription’s most repeatable line.

## 6. Voice and point of view

- **A reader who works with machines:** Can notice the memorial’s causal sequence without supplying technical expertise as fact.
- **A person uneasy with blame:** Questions whether the final lesson turns an unknown failure into a moral fault.
- **A keeper of the wording:** Wants the deaths remembered without making technical details up.

The people listed here are proposed present-day readers, not canon characters and not witnesses unless another verified source establishes that history. Keep the original memorial voice distinct from any reply written for this plan.

## 7. Placement and current reachability

The exact local anchor is memorial_power_failure/cold_took_them. MemorialSystem owns survivor memorialization, grief, mourning, and eulogies, but this separate data record’s loader and player-facing consumer have not been verified. Before content placement, confirm the current schema, owner, route, and presentation context. Catalog presence alone proves no playable route. This plan contributes no new route or content registry.

## 8. Player agency

The player may read, ask who authored a claim, hear a second interpretation, decline to discuss it, or leave without a verdict. These are editorial postures rather than promised controls. Silence must remain a complete response. Do not attach approval, trust, reputation, item, resource, treatment, memorial completion, or relationship state to the reader’s interpretation.

## 9. Continuity, dignity, and safety

Do not specify generator type, failure cause, power grid, repair tools, procedure, shelter temperature, exposure duration, or individual responsibility. Give no repair or cold-weather survival advice. Do not treat “never neglect the machines” as proof of negligence by a named person or as a new maintenance mechanic.
 Keep the source statement, a proposed speaker’s interpretation, and any verified world fact visibly separate. Avoid reenacting death, assigning fault without evidence, or converting grief into a tutorial.

## 11. Narrative sequence

These six editorial movements organize the prose bank; they are not six required visits, a chronology, or a quest chain. A verified consumer may use a single beat, a short excerpt, or none of the candidates.

### Movement 1: The Generator Failed

The first clause names an event but no mechanism. In this movement the candidate question is: How does a reader resist completing the missing technical explanation? The memorial’s phrase “the generator failed” gives the reader a concrete point of attention, while the record remains a public statement rather than a complete witness account. The scene should let people disagree about meaning without asking the player to approve one interpretation.

### Movement 2: Cold as Consequence

The memorial connects a machine and a human outcome in one sentence. In this movement the candidate question is: What can sequence suggest without proving causality in detail? The memorial’s phrase “the cold took them” gives the reader a concrete point of attention, while the record remains a public statement rather than a complete witness account. The scene should let people disagree about meaning without asking the player to approve one interpretation.

### Movement 3: We Tried to Repair It

The plural remembers effort, not a repair log. In this movement the candidate question is: How can effort be honored without inventing tools or steps? The memorial’s phrase “we tried to repair it” gives the reader a concrete point of attention, while the record remains a public statement rather than a complete witness account. The scene should let people disagree about meaning without asking the player to approve one interpretation.

### Movement 4: It Was Too Late

The memorial leaves timing compressed. In this movement the candidate question is: Who can claim to know when a different outcome stopped being possible? The memorial’s phrase “it was too late” gives the reader a concrete point of attention, while the record remains a public statement rather than a complete witness account. The scene should let people disagree about meaning without asking the player to approve one interpretation.

### Movement 5: Their Deaths Remind Us

The dead are made to carry a warning for the living. In this movement the candidate question is: When does a lesson become a burden placed on the dead? The memorial’s phrase “their deaths remind us” gives the reader a concrete point of attention, while the record remains a public statement rather than a complete witness account. The scene should let people disagree about meaning without asking the player to approve one interpretation.

### Movement 6: Machines That Keep Us Alive

The closing language turns equipment into a moral responsibility. In this movement the candidate question is: How can care for equipment be remembered without assigning unsupported blame? The memorial’s phrase “never neglect” gives the reader a concrete point of attention, while the record remains a public statement rather than a complete witness account. The scene should let people disagree about meaning without asking the player to approve one interpretation.

## 12. Beat bank and alternative passage drafts

Every movement meets each of eight source phrases. The four forms per pairing are alternatives: an observable scene, an attributable reader-authored fragment, a two-voice conversation, and a conditional return. Their headings help writers compare form; they do not promise that all versions fit one consumer. Dialogue is deliberately from proposed present readers, not from the person memorialized.

### Beat 01: The Generator Failed × the generator failed

**Beat question:** When a reader meets “the generator failed” during “The Generator Failed,” what can they honor in that wording while remembering that an event in the text with no specified component or cause?

#### Scene draft 001 — the generator failed — The Generator Failed

This version starts in the small interval between recognizing the record and deciding what to repeat from it. The dramatic work of “The Generator Failed” comes from placing “the generator failed” beside what the memorial does not say. No invented detail is allowed to close that gap. The proposed reader is not a witness to the life being memorialized; their presence only gives the source wording a present audience.

Let the focus settle on “the generator failed”. The phrase has force because an event in the text with no specified component or cause. A second person notices that force before anyone turns it into an explanation. They may look toward the line, then toward the person who has stopped reading, giving the beat a human action rather than an invented flashback.

A reader who refuses to turn sequence into blame begins with a modest response: “I can read the generator failed, but I cannot tell from this sentence everything that happened. I want to keep the wording because it matters to someone, and I want to leave room where the record leaves room.” The line is written as present interpretation, not a quotation falsely attributed to the person on the memorial.

A keeper who lets the final imperative remain contested replies, “Then let us ask what it asks of us now. When a reader meets “the generator failed” during “The Generator Failed,” what can they honor in that wording while remembering that an event in the text with no specified component or cause? I can honor the claim and still notice what it does not tell us.” The answer should be specific to the source phrase; it must not flatten the disagreement into a lesson about all grief or all survivors.

A third voice, a careful machine worker who names the limits of their knowledge, may interrupt gently. They do not correct the catalog or add evidence. They ask whether the word “the generator failed” is being repeated because it comforts the room, because it warns the room, or because no one knows what else to say. Let more than one motive remain possible.

The scene’s emotional turn around “the generator failed” in “The Generator Failed” is small: one listener stops trying to finish the missing story for a moment. A chair shifts, a copied line is turned face down and then face up again, or the speaker chooses a less certain verb. The selected gesture should fit the verified presentation context; no physical memorial site is established by this proposal.

For “the generator failed” in “The Generator Failed,” player-facing tone stays open. The player may read, ask for the source wording again, decline the conversation, or simply listen. These are writing possibilities, not a promised menu or branching system. No answer earns approval, trust, resources, safety, reputation, or a new relationship state.

The closing breath belongs to the living reader; do not fabricate an answer on behalf of the person commemorated. The last attention returns to “the generator failed”, now heard through the live disagreement rather than treated as a complete biography.

**Drafting focus.** Keep the reader’s attention on “the generator failed” rather than on a newly invented biography. The writer may ask When a reader meets “the generator failed” during “The Generator Failed,” what can they honor in that wording while remembering that an event in the text with no specified component or cause? The answer should emerge through voice, hesitation, and what the speakers decline to claim; it should not be delivered by an omniscient narrator. This restraint is part of the content, not a substitute for dramatic movement.

## 13. Player-facing tone

Keep sentences close to the source wording and the present reader’s actual knowledge. Allow one voice to value the memorial and another to object to its universal-sounding conclusion. Neither reaction should be framed as the correct morality. Use quiet material action rather than explanatory narration. Avoid turning illness, hunger, conflict, radiation, cold, machinery, duty, or trust into a puzzle tutorial or instruction to the player.

## 14. Editorial acceptance

Do not specify generator type, failure cause, power grid, repair tools, procedure, shelter temperature, exposure duration, or individual responsibility. Give no repair or cold-weather survival advice. Do not treat “never neglect the machines” as proof of negligence by a named person or as a new maintenance mechanic.
 Re-check the exact local record and any newer narrative data before selecting text. Retain only passages that the current consumer can reach and attribute honestly. Shortening a candidate is preferable to implying unsupported canon.

## 15. Character and relationship continuity

The proposed roles are anonymous present-day readers. Do not promote them into named survivors, historical witnesses, patients, faction leaders, victims, or permanent companions without a separate source and authorized content decision. Their relationships exist only within the selected text. Gratitude, skepticism, grief, forgiveness, caution, and disagreement do not become saved relationship values.

## 16. Passage-selection map

**Use a scene** when a verified reader-facing owner can stage a present response without claiming a sourced location. **Use a record** when existing content supports authorship and provenance. **Use a conversation** when a second perspective adds a genuine question rather than repeating the thesis. **Use a return vignette** only if the existing route already supports the callback condition; otherwise use its non-conditional final line or omit it. These are writing choices, not feature requests.
